#2804. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2801. Boxing chiefs to assess Fury before Joshua fight following schizophrenia comments

The British Boxing Board of Control will assess Tyson Fury ahead of his hotly anticipated bout against Anthony Joshua following comments he made about hearing voices during a mental health episode.

A new self-titled documentary about the 38-year-old, to be released on Netflix on Friday, goes into detail about his mental health struggles. He and his wife Paris recalled frightening episodes in which he heard voices threatening him and his family after he was hospitalised with a suspected heart attack in 2016.

He said: "Words can't explain the darkness I was in. It was like the living version of the pits of hell's fire. I 100 per cent thought I'd end up in a padded room with the door locked and that scared me so much."

In the original version of the documentary, which has since been edited before release, Paris said he was "mild schizophrenic", although he was not medically diagnosed. Doctors said he had experienced a severe anxiety attack.

Paris clarified her comments to The Mirror on Thursday: "Whilst Tyson did show some symptoms associated with schizophrenia, he doesn't have this condition and has never received a diagnosis. Mental health is complex, and it isn't always easy to find the right words, especially when you're living it with someone you love."

The British Boxing Board of Control said it would evaluate the comments broadcast in the documentary before clearing Fury to box Joshua, with the fight scheduled for 11 December in Cardiff's Principality Stadium.

The board's general secretary, Robert Smith, told BBC Sport: "We are in the process of dealing with the matter but if there are any medical issues we wouldn't let anyone box, doesn't matter if it's Tyson Fury or not, it could be anybody.

"I'm fairly confident he will be absolutely fine. We're dealing with it now and we'll have an answer very shortly.

"If our doctors are satisfied he's fit to box, he will box."

He declined to comment on the specifics of the process, saying, "it's a medical matter".

The board's regulations state that schizophrenia is among psychological conditions which "may preclude" a boxer from obtaining a first licence. An existing licence holder is subject to "such action as in [the board's] absolute discretion it sees fit".

#2800. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2799. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

#2798. Junk bonds are 'flashing yellow.' Watch these warning signs

Cracks are forming in the junk bond market as investors demand higher payouts for owning the market's riskiest debt. It isn't time to ditch high-yield bonds, but investors should pay attention to the warning signs.

High-yield bonds now yield 8.1%, up from 7.22% a month ago. The increase reflects a jump in yields across the curve as investors bake in more inflation from high energy prices and other pressures, including concern about the deficit — hitting nearly $2 trillion in the fiscal year that ended Sept. 30.

The high-yield market is also showing stress on the credit side with spreads recently widening to levels not seen since April, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of St. Louis. Credit spreads are the difference in yield between the bonds and Treasurys of similar maturities. Wider spreads mean investors are demanding higher yields for holding corporate debt, viewing it as riskier.

Spreads are at 315 basis points in the overall high-yield market, higher than a year ago but still below levels in March when they reached 346 bps. One basis point equals one one-hundredth of a percent, or 0.01%.

The high-yield market consists of bonds rated BB+ by S&P and Fitch and Ba1 and under by Moody's. The lowest-rated cohort, CCC and below, has seen the most movement with spreads climbing dramatically over the past year to roughly 1,250 bps.

Right now, the high-yield market is "flashing yellow" but is "far from red," said Michael Arone, chief investment strategist at State Street Investment Management.

It makes sense that investors are demanding more compensation for taking on additional credit risk as borrowing costs rise, he said.

Yields are elevated across the board with the 10-year Treasury reaching its highest level since 2002 earlier in the week.

"The bigger question is whether this is simply a repricing of interest rate risk, or the beginning of a more fundamental reassessment of credit quality," Arone said.

He's in a wait-and-see mode since earnings are still growing, interest-coverage ratios remain good, and while default rates have ticked up some, he believes it is not concerning.

That said, the starting point in spreads is likely weighing on investors' psyche, since they are still low by historical standards.

"There's a small margin of error here, which I also think raises the anxiety level," Arone explained. "The compensation that investors are receiving for taking on this credit risk isn't overwhelming relative to history, and therefore subtle changes in credit spreads can be concerning."

While there may be concerns about part of the lower-rated market, the overall high-yield market is in good fundamental shape.

In fact, credit quality is at a record high, with BB bonds making up over 60% of the market compared with 38% prior to the global financial crisis, said Kelley Gerrity, a fixed income strategist at Morgan Stanley Investment Management.

"We've had higher-quality companies coming in, and with higher rates now, you also have more discipline from companies that are more indebted ... just because of the higher cost of capital, so that actually is creating a bit of a healthier picture as well," she said.

The lowest tier of high yield has always been more speculative, since the bonds have a higher default risk. Therefore, it's no surprise that they are the ones seeing the most widening in spreads, said Collin Martin, head of fixed income research and strategy at the Schwab Center for Financial Research.

The overall move in high-yield spreads have been "orderly," he said. "Are there cracks forming? I think the cracks are logical cracks with the lowest-rated bonds and it's too early to say that it's spreading to the broad credit market."

In addition, fluctuations within the CCC-rated and lower spreads have been idiosyncratic, Gerrity added. Morgan Stanley recently broke down the spreads in the lowest cohort into two buckets: performing assets and non-performing assets, which are defined as spreads above 1,000 bps.

The non-performing spread to worst is 2,818 bps, while the performing bucket — the biggest piece of the CCC market — is 461 bps, she said.

"It is isolated," she said. "I'm not necessarily sure that that will go away anytime soon, but it doesn't tell the story of overall concern and flashing caution for credit markets right now."

For now, investors should remain selective within higher yield, Gerrity advised.

"We're looking for the best relative value opportunities, and we think that exists within that single-B cohort, and so we're leaning into that," she said.

Investors should be concerned if there is a steep widening in the broader high-yield market.

But there's scant evidence of stress in the BB cohort. Spreads in that group are at 194 bps, up from 179 bps a year ago — although the move hasn't been in a straight line.

"We're going to be focusing our attention on what are considered some stronger businesses," Martin said. "If we start to see the markets demand higher spreads there also, that's what we'll be looking at to see if risks are really rising."

It's also important to understand that the Federal Reserve is raising rates in a strong economic environment, said R.J. Gallo, chief investment officer of global fixed income at Federated Hermes. The central bank is concerned about an inflation problem amid high fossil fuel prices due to the Iran war, while growth has been stronger than expected, he noted.

"With growth being good as the reason that the Fed is hiking, then you wouldn't expect high yield to blow out because growth means revenues stay up, means cash flow and profitability stays up," he explained.

High yield becomes a disaster when the economy is heading into a sharp economic downturn, Gallo said.

"That's when spreads really widen out," he said. "But a recession is not the odds-on bet. Now, talk to me in six months if the Fed keeps hiking over and over, if oil prices stay high for longer, well, then maybe we will start to wonder."

#2795. Lakers, behind Luka Doncic, must crack Wemby code, exploit these areas to beat Spurs

The stars are aligning for a special regular-season game between the Lakers and Spurs on Friday night in San Antonio.

It's two of the NBA's best teams and the Western Conference's top teams matching up for the first time this season, featuring the top early-season MVP candidates in Lakers guard Luka Dončić and Spurs big man Victor Wembanyama.

Luka Doncic of the Los Angeles Lakers shoots a free throw. NBAE via Getty Images

And it's also, essentially, a win-or-go-home NBA Group Play game for West Group C.

For the Lakers, the Spurs will be the biggest test for this still-newish group, with the reigning Western Conference champions equipped to poke the holes that most of the Lakers' previous opponents haven't been able to.

But the Lakers are also equipped to test the Spurs in ways not every team is able to.

And it starts with Dončić — one of the league's best offensive players who can force the Spurs to have to adjust their defensive strategy of keeping Wembanyama around the basket as much as possible.

Even amid a shooting slump, the Slovenian superstar's pull-up shooting and playmaking force defenses to be more aggressive with their coverages when he's the ball handler in pick and rolls.

San Antonio Spurs center Victor Wembanyama walks up the court. AP Photo/Darren Abate

Or even when he's isolating his defender, with opponents often showing proactive help to crowd driving and passing lanes.

If Austin Reaves is available against the Spurs after missing the previous five games due to a bruised left heel, the Spurs won't be able to load up their defense on Dončić in the same way.

The Spurs' defensive scheme allows for their perimeter defenders to be more aggressive with the intent of funneling actions toward Wembanyama, who is the league's best rim protector in large part due to his 7-foot-4 frame.

But the Lakers' use of Dončić starting possessions off the ball before receiving a screen to get the ball can test the Spurs' preference.

Austin Reaves (15) of the Los Angeles Lakers clapping. NBAE via Getty Images

If Wembanyama is too far in a drop and Dončić's defender doesn't stay attached, Dončić has the room he needs to get his shot off.

Wembanyama has the size and agility to play closer to the level of the screen while being in position to recover to protect the rim.

It's a cat-and-mouse game Dončić is just as equipped as any player to win against the Spurs, who once again have one of the league's best defenses.

Another area the Lakers are equipped to exploit is the Spurs' stingy transition defense.

The Spurs are one of the best teams at preventing scoring on fast breaks, but the Lakers are one of the best. They'll have to remain consistent with their throwahead passes without turning the ball over.

And defensively, Walker Kessler empowers the Lakers to prioritize protecting the paint against Wembanyama and forcing the Spurs, who are shooting 33.7% on 3-pointers (No. 25), to beat them with outside shots.

Friday will be a big test for the Lakers.

But they're equipped to come out of the matchup better than expected.

#2792. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

#2791. Leeds United Chase French Striker Rennes Could Sell: Is £39m Worth It?

Leeds United and Everton are reportedly targeting Rennes striker Esteban Lepaul, according to Sport Witness.

The 26-year-old France international scored 20 goals and provided five assists in Ligue 1 last season, then added seven in eight league appearances this term, four of them penalties. Rennes extended his contract to 2030 in June, yet the report says they are preparing for a January or summer exit. Transfermarkt values him at €45m, roughly £39m.

Eliezer Mayenda, a €22m (about £19m) signing, is struggling, so Rennes are already scouting Rosenborg's Amin Chiakha as a replacement in attack.

FotMob's data shows a penalty-box finisher. His 29 shots have produced 16 on target and 5.80 expected goals, and he ranks in the 95th percentile among forwards for goals.

The weaknesses are just as clear. He wins 40.9% of his aerial duels and 37.8% of all duels, and sits in the third percentile for defensive contributions. Only three of his seven goals this season came from open play.

Leeds scored only 10 goals in their first ten league games before beating Chelsea 4-1 last Saturday to reach 17 points from 11. Noah Okafor scored twice there and Dominic Calvert-Lewin, 29, converted a penalty, yet the striker missed 19 big chances in the Premier League last season, according to FotMob.

Farke's back three and two strikers need forwards who press, and Calvert-Lewin has led the Premier League for pressures applied since the start of last season. Lepaul's defensive numbers would test that.

ISTANBUL, TURKEY – AUGUST 2: Anthony Rouault of Rennes interacts with his teammate Esteban Lepaul during the during the pre-season friendly match between Galatasaray SK and Rennes at Rams Park Stadium on August 2, 2026 in Istanbul, Turkey. (Photo by Ahmad Mora/Getty Images)

No, at least not in January and not at £39m or more. Rennes can keep him in Europa League football on a contract until 2030, and TEAMtalk reports Everton lead the chase.

Everton have only Thierno Barry as a senior centre-forward, but Leeds face no such shortage. Their problem is conversion, which makes Lepaul tempting, yet he created just seven chances in 562 minutes and would not help Farke's pressing. Calvert-Lewin started each of Leeds' first ten league games, and Okafor is scoring, so Lepaul would arrive as a rotation option at a starter's price.

A cheaper, pressing-minded forward would suit the back three better. Summer is the sensible window, when Rennes may have Chiakha in place and Calvert-Lewin's age gives succession a stronger case. Paying about £39m for 20 goals is fair only if the player can also defend from the front, and Lepaul's numbers say he cannot yet.

#2789. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Uncertainty in the Middle East, elevated Treasury yields and fears about an AI bubble continue to keep global markets on edge. Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking stable income can consider enhancing their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

However, the vast universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Tracking the ratings of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as their recommendations are backed by in-depth research.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Chord Energy is an independent exploration and production company with long-lived assets primarily in the Williston Basin. With a quarterly base dividend of $1.30 per share, CHRD offers a dividend yield of 3.67%.

In a research note on third-quarter expectations, RBC Capital analyst Scott Hanold reiterated a buy rating on CHRD stock with a price target of $175. "We model production near the top of guidance and continue to see strong performance from new wells and base production," said Hanold.

Specifically, the five-star analyst expects Q3 2026 production at 283 thousand barrels of oil equivalent per day, or Mboe/d, in line with the Street's consensus and near the high end of the 278 to 284 Mboe/d guidance. Hanold said his Q3 2026 earnings per share estimate increased to $5.29 due to lower non-cash depreciation, depletion and amortization.

Meanwhile, cash flow per share estimate moved lower to $13.59 to reflect Hanold's update based on the current backdrop and commodity mark-to-market. He expects Chord Energy to report capital spending of $375 million for Q3 2026, slightly above the Street's estimate of $369 million.

Overall, Hanold remains bullish on CHRD, given its best-in-class balance sheet. He expects a free cash flow payout of 80% in the third quarter, with the possibility of an upside. Hanold expects investors to focus on management commentary on 2027 capital plans, Williston M&A opportunities, and long-term performance of 4-mile wells.

Hanold ranks No. 310 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 14.9%. See Chord Energy KPIs on TipRanks.

Williams, an energy infrastructure company that delivers natural gas, is the next dividend pick. The company recently completed the $5.5 billion acquisition of Momentum Midstream, which will expand its natural gas infrastructure in the Haynesville to meet growing Gulf Coast LNG, power and industrial demand. At a quarterly dividend of $0.5250 per share, Williams offers a yield of 2.89%.

In her latest research note, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $87. Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, the analyst said that she expects WMB to announce strong quarterly performance.

Scotto raised her estimates to reflect insights from the quarterly catch-up call, the completion of the Momentum Midstream acquisition, and mark-to-market quarter-to-date commodity prices. She expects WMB to report Q3 2026 adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of $2,036 million, which is modestly above the Street's consensus estimate.

Furthermore, Scotto expects Williams' Transmission, Power & Gulf segment to increase quarter-over-quarter, driven by the startup of the Socrates power project and favorable natural gas liquids, or NGL, margins.

Scotto noted that WMB stock has underperformed due to backlash related to data centers and investor anticipation of another Power Innovation project. She expects the company to announce at least one more project before the end of this year, potentially with a new customer and a contract duration of 15 to 20 years, which would be a positive catalyst for the stock.

Scotto ranks No. 175 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been profitable 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.2%. See Williams Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

EOG Resources, a crude oil and natural gas exploration and production company, will hold its third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 6. At a quarterly dividend of $1.02 per share, EOG offers a dividend yield of 2.75%.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Jefferies analyst Lloyd Byrne reiterated a buy rating on EOG Resources stock and increased his price target to $185 from $175. The analyst expects the company's Q3 cash flow per share to exceed the Street's consensus by 13%, driven by better-than-expected production.

Byrne expects crude oil production, excluding the UAE, to reach 551 Mboe/d, which is at the high end of guidance (546-551 Mboe/d) and above the Street's projection of 548 Mboe/d, driven by continued growth in the Utica.

Furthermore, Byrne expects cash operating expenses to come in at $10.27 per barrel of oil equivalent, below the midpoint of guidance of $10.05 to $11.35, helped by lower lease operating expenses. Overall, the analyst expects EOG to deliver free cash flow of $2.65 billion, supporting another quarter of buybacks near the minimum 70% shareholder return threshold and implying about $1.5 billion in share repurchases.

"As a first indication of the '27 plan, we expect EOG will message another year of mid-single-digit (~3.5%) growth on a flat capital budget (~$6.55bn), which is above the Street modeling 1% oil growth at $6.6bn," said Byrne.

Byrne ranks No. 303 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 18.5%. See EOG Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

#2788. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for smaller loan exemption from appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Consumer Financial Protection Bureau, the Federal Reserve Board, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that the 2027 threshold for higher-priced mortgage loans that are subject to special appraisal requirements will increase from $34,200 to $35,500.

The threshold amount will be effective January 1, 2027, and is based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W, as of June 1, 2026.

The Dodd-Frank Act added special appraisal requirements for higher-priced mortgage loans to the Truth in Lending Act, including that creditors obtain a written appraisal based on a physical visit to the interior of the home before making a higher-priced mortgage loan. The rules implementing these requirements contain an exemption for loans at or below a threshold amount that is adjusted annually to reflect CPI-W increases.

#2786. Bucks vs. Nets Player Grades: Turner, Burries, and rest of team come down to earth in dreadful showing

In what might have been the worst Bucks game I've watched in some time—which is saying something—the Milwaukee Bucks lost to the 3-13 Brooklyn Nets 104-97. It really felt like the Bucks got the monkey off their backs against the Raptors and came into this game without a care in the world. They didn't play as a team, nor did they play with any passion. The worst part is that there aren't going to be many games this season where the Bucks have a genuine 50-50 chance of winning, and for Milwaukee to put up that kind of performance against a Brooklyn team that really tried its hardest to lose was really disappointing. The game also brings the Bucks to 0-3 in the NBA Cup. Read our full summary of the game here and catch a six-minute audio recap on the Bucks+ podcast, Bucks In Six Minutes, below.

37 minutes, 28 points, 3 rebounds, 5 assists, 3 steals, 5 turnovers, 10/20 FG, 3/9 3P, 5/5 FT, 0

I mean, Herro was just about the team's only source of creation, so I do want to credit him for that. Overall, though, this wasn't all that impressive of a game. I didn't think Herro was as willing to get his teammates involved as he had been, settling for a fair few bad shots and dominating the ball too much; granted, he would have had a few more assists if anyone could make a shot, but alas. And then the turnovers, man; most of them felt like killer live-ball giveaways.

18 minutes, 2 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 0 turnovers, 0/7 FG, 0/4 3P, -7

Oof. Really did not like Rollins' game. He made some nice reads to open the third, but overall, most of his shots came after he dribbled the air out of the ball for 10-14 seconds, then settled for step-back threes or wild drives at the rim. It was almost as if Rollins was trying to force his way into a groove, rather than playing instinctively and letting opportunities come to him. Moreover, we can usually rely on Rollins' defence being solid, but even that part of his game felt off; there was one possession in the third where the Bucks had finally taken the lead, were in a groove, and Rollins was guarding someone on the right wing; he was in perfect position to contain the ball, but he lunged for the steal, didn't get it, and his man went right past him, leading to the open three. Absolute coach killer.

31 minutes, 13 points, 3 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 turnover, 5/11 FG, 3/7 3P, -12

Not too many complaints about Kuzma's game. He was just about Milwaukee's only credible defender on Randle, and overall, I thought he did a solid job making the Nets veteran work. Also made some solid reads to get into the paint and then spray out to shooters.

34 minutes, 21 points, 4 rebounds, 4 assists, 2 steals, 9/15 FG, 1/5 3P, 2/4 FT, +5

Jaquez was solid in the middle game and a key part of the Bucks' best runs. He finished well around the rim and got his teammates involved. Still, can't go 1/5 from three when it feels like most of them are wide open. Just can't happen.

25 minutes, 3 points, 6 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 steal, 3 blocks, 3 turnovers, 1/2 FG, 1/2 3P, -1

Obviously, people will look at the scoring numbers, but I would say Turner's lack of production was a byproduct of the Bucks' lack of ball movement. Against the Raptors, the ball was moving, and that's how he got his opportunities; against the Nets, everything was stagnant and slow, so Turner shot just twice. The big man still impacted the game in other ways, but he also had a few backbreaking live-ball turnovers. Overall, it wasn't a good game for Turner by any means, but I thought other factors also contributed to him not playing well.

22 minutes, 13 points, 14 rebounds, 2 assists, 2 blocks, 1 turnover, 6/10 FG, 1/2 3P, -6

On balance, Ware was probably one of Milwaukee's better players. He created extra opportunities, with five of his 14 rebounds coming on the offensive glass, and he would have scored more points if just a few of those tip-ins had gone in. Also, continues to rack up blocks and be a presence around the rim (on both ends). I will say that the Nets were happy to play much higher in their pick-and-roll coverage with Ware in the game because they didn't trust his decision-making with a numbers advantage; he has to improve in that facet of the game.

21 minutes, 3 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 2 turnovers, 1/6 FG, 0/2 3P, 1/2 FT, -4

Burries ended his hot streak with a stinker. He couldn't find the same creases he had been finding (which is weird against one of the league's worst defensive teams), and he also didn't impact in other areas the way he usually does. Burries also made a really odd turnover in the fourth quarter: he caught the ball with no awareness that MPJ was right there and got it ripped away; part of that was on Ware for passing him the ball there, but Burries needs to have better awareness.

21 minutes, 8 points, 3 rebounds, 1 turnover, 4 PF, 3/6 FG, 2/5 3P, 0

I mean, Ament got most of his looks off catch-and-shoot opportunities, but he also got handed a few grenades as the shot clock was winding down. The rook was the third defender (behind Kuzma and Jaquez) on Randle, and despite a major weight disadvantage, he was decent in that matchup; Ament clearly read the scout, forcing him to his right hand and into help as much as possible—good stuff. He had a few possessions where he rotated late from the weak side in help, though.

12 minutes, 4 points, 4 rebounds, 3 assists, 2/7 FG, 0/5 3P, +2

Weird minutes for Jakucionis, who played three minutes in the first half and then was thrown into the fire at the end without really getting a feel for the game. He missed all his threes, but most were the right shots to take (the one bad one being his corner three, where he had Herro wide open for the extra pass up top). Also got baited into a bad foul late as the Bucks were making a charge.

11 minutes, 2 points, 2 assists, 1/2 FG, -6

I don't know. I guess LeVert was fine in his minutes. As I'll get to below, my issue was with how much he played.

This was the first game where I truly felt Jenkins played a key role in the team losing, mainly because of rotations. He opened the second quarter with a Rollins-Green-LeVert-Kuzma-Turner lineup that had a -78.6 net rating in 3:18, with Rollins—who, news flash, has been struggling—being the only creator (and boy did he try to create!). Then, to start the fourth quarter, Jenkins threw out a Jakucionis-Burries-LeVert-Ament-Ware lineup that had a -40.0 net rating in 2:38. One downside to starting all the veterans—a unit that, to their credit, played well together in this one—is that you can eventually get to lineups like these. Granted, I get that with all of this you're robbing Peter to pay Paul to some degree: if you take one guy from one lineup to help another, that original lineup will lose something. Even so, Jenkins can strike a much better balance than he did last night.

DNP-CD: Jericho Sims, Ousmane Dieng, Bogoljub Markovic, Gary Trent Jr.

Inactive: Pete Nance, Cormac Ryan, Kam Jones,

Even the Bucks can get high on their own supply and, dare I say it, disrespect their opponent.

Jenkins: "We abandoned what was working for us in the first half. We didn't have rhythm to our offence. I guess we got bored with the stuff that was working well for us."

Kuzma: "I think we were just thinking for ourselves… How can I get myself going instead of how can I get the team going? That's the story of our season right now."

I can't stress enough how much the Nets tried to give this game to the Bucks. They shot 41.6% from the field and 26.3% from three. Michael Porter Jr. went 4/14, for goodness' sake; you have to win when that happens. It felt like the team entered this game thinking they'd get a win; they were playing a team just as bad, and another win would come easy while they all played for their own numbers.

The Bucks had 14 turnovers for the game, and nine came in the last quarter.

Assistant coach Brad Jones noted at halftime that all 47 of the Bucks' first-half points came after touching the paint in that possession—every one.

I don't get the LeVert minutes sometimes. Like, if he stands out in his first stint, sure, throw him out there for another; but if his first stint doesn't stand out in any way, why put him back in? Surely you'd rather give those minutes to Dieng or even a guard. Sometimes the rotations can feel incredibly random.

The Bucks head to Detroit on Friday to face the Pistons, kicking off Milwaukee's back-to-back, with OKC in town the following night. Catch the game at 6:30 p.m. CST on MY24.

#2785. Agencies announce dollar thresholds for applicability of truth in lending and consumer leasing rules for consumer credit and lease transactions

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 3:00 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board and the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau today announced the dollar thresholds used to determine whether certain consumer credit and lease transactions in 2027 are subject to certain protections under Regulation Z (Truth in Lending) and Regulation M (Consumer Leasing).

By law, the agencies are required to adjust the thresholds annually based on the annual percentage increase in the Consumer Price Index for Urban Wage Earners and Clerical Workers, known as CPI-W. Transactions at or below the thresholds are subject to the protections of the regulations.

Specifically, based on the 3.9 percent annual percentage increase in the CPI-W as of June 1, 2026, Regulation Z and Regulation M generally will apply to consumer credit transactions and consumer leases of $76,300 or less in 2027. However, private education loans and loans secured by real property, such as mortgages, are subject to Regulation Z regardless of the amount of the loan.

Federal Register notice: Consumer Leasing (Regulation M) (PDF)

Federal Register notice: Truth in Lending (Regulation Z) (PDF)

#2784. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2783. NFL picks: Who the experts predict to win in the Broncos-Steelers game

The Denver Broncos (8-2) will be looking for their first victory in Pittsburgh since 2006 when they take on the Steelers (6-4) on Black Friday. They will also look to stay ahead of the Kansas City Chiefs, who play on Thanksgiving, in the race for first place in the AFC West.

The Broncos have not lost since Week 4, winning each of their last six games to put them on the same kind of pace they were on last year at this time, when they ended up with the No. 1 seed in the AFC. Defeating a Steelers team starting its backup quarterback following a disastrous week seems like a must for Denver.

How do the experts see this one shaking out? Let's take a look.

"This Friday game pits two teams who would be in the playoffs right now," wrote Prisco for CBS. "But the Steelers have lost two straight and haven't looked good. Denver has won six in a row. But I think the Steelers will find a way to show up and win this one behind their running game. It won't be pretty, but the Steelers will find a way."

"The Steelers are in a tough spot here after back-to-back losses in the AFC North race," wrote Bender for The Sporting News. "Aaron Rodgers averages 180 passing yards the last two weeks, and he's taken eight sacks. Jonah Coleman is coming off a 113-yard, three-TD performance for the Broncos. Pittsburgh is 4-0 S/U at home this season, and the Steelers deliver at home."

EDIT: This prediction was made before the Steelers benched and then released Aaron Rodgers.

"The Broncos are starting to put it together on offense, while the Steelers' offense is falling apart," wrote Cluff for Arizona Republic. "Jonah Coleman and Denver will extend their win streak to seven games with a big win on the road, handing Pittsburgh a third straight loss and its first loss at home this season."

"Forget rolling back prices -- roll back your expectations for Pittsburgh on Black Friday. It would be bad enough if the Steelers were only dealing with a miserable slump over the past two weeks, during which they've been outscored 37-6 in the first half of consecutive losses. But Pittsburgh also must prepare to face an 8-2 Broncos squad amid a soap operatic couple of days that included the benching of Aaron Rodgers late Tuesday night; Rodgers going on The Pat McAfee Show on Wednesday to give his version of events before declaring he would not be returning to the team; and ultimately, the Steelers' release of the 42-year old quarterback. Those events have created a uniquely disruptive environment while Pittsburgh prepares to play Denver on a short week. Mason Rudolph will now serve as the Steelers' starter, but he's a known entity at this point of his career. The 31-year-old has a 9-9-1 record with 4,927 passing yards, 30 touchdowns and 22 interceptions. He has not been the type to elevate players to a degree necessary to take down a Super Bowl contender. The Broncos seemingly have multiple routes to a victory: They could certainly end up blowing out a team dealing with turmoil, but even if the Steelers rise to the occasion, Pittsburgh lacks the offensive punch to pull away from Denver. Should it end up close late, the Broncos are very comfortable with that script. Through 10 games this season, Bo Nix already has a league-leading five fourth-quarter comebacks and six game-winning drives."

The rest of the panel at NFL.com, Brooke Cersosimo, Gennaro Filice, Dan Parr and Jeremy Bergman all chose the Broncos as well.

Brook Lopez playing in an NBA game for the Clippers against the Mavericks. NBAE via Getty Images

#2749. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2748. North Korea's Kim calls for nuclear arsenal expansion, says status 'will never change'

North Korean leader Kim Jong Un called for bolstering the country's nuclear arsenal and expanding the use of tactical nuclear weapons across multiple domains, state media reported Thursday, as Pyongyang reaffirmed its determination to retain its nuclear arms.

Kim's comments followed a working visit Wednesday to facilities involved in nuclear material production and weapons research, according to the Korean Central News Agency.

At the Nuclear Weapons Institute, he urged greater attention to "operationalizing" tactical nuclear weapons for a range of missions.

Kim directly linked the development of those weapons to North Korea "sharing a border with its most dangerous, hostile state," an apparent reference to South Korea.

The visit was part of Pyongyang's efforts to counter what Kim described as "the reckless expansion of adversary states' nuclear forces and military alliances," according to KCNA. He also singled out South Korea as a threat to North Korea's security and sovereignty.

Kim reiterated that North Korea's status as a nuclear-armed state was irreversible, dismissing "denuclearization rhetoric" and saying its nuclear status "will never change."

"[Our nuclear policy] is a core element of safeguarding the sovereignty of the Republic, enshrined in the supreme law of the state," he added.

The report did not identify the sites Kim visited or disclose any production figures.

The announcement followed an assessment last week by the International Atomic Energy Agency that another extension to Yongbyon's original uranium-enrichment facility had been externally completed by late October.

The nuclear watchdog has lacked access to North Korea since 2009 and monitors its activities using satellite imagery and open-source intelligence.

Citing what he portrayed as growing threats from Washington and its allies, Kim accused the U.S. of encouraging neighboring allies to acquire nuclear-powered submarines, pursuing nuclear sharing and the joint use of nuclear weapons.

His comments come after South Korea announced efforts to acquire nuclear-powered submarines after President Donald Trump backed the plan in October 2025, according to a White House joint fact sheet. Nuclear-powered propulsion does not itself mean that a vessel carries nuclear weapons.

Trump had expressed interest in meeting with Kim at the APEC summit in Shenzhen last week, according to Reuters, but no meeting took place and North Korea did not publicly respond to the proposal.

Trump denied that Kim had ignored him, adding that communications could "start up right away," in an interview with the New York Post Tuesday. He said the two leaders had not been in contact "recently."

"We've been off to other things," Trump added.

North Korea has continued to accelerate its nuclear weapons development and rebuff South Korean and U.S. attempts to resume dialogue since negotiations between Trump and Kim collapsed in 2019.

— CNBC's Jenny Lee contributed to this report.

#2747. So much for a Spurs fan to be thankful for in 2026 and beyond

My first Thanksgiving post for Pounding the Rock was on November 27, 2014, about a month after I joined the team. (The Pounding the Rock team, not the Spurs.) I didn't write about being thankful for the Redemption Finals, even thought we were all VERY thankful for, and the joy that win brought all basketball fans. Instead, I wrote about a conversation I had with NBA great Rudy Tomjanovich earlier that month:

"Especially in light of everything going on in the sports world, I loved to hear Rudy T's comments about the team we all root for. He used the words "good people". Among all the things to be thankful for this Thanksgiving, the list includes the ability to cheer on a Spurs team that is a group of good people. Not everyone can say that, but we can. Happy Thanksgiving, Spurs Nation."

I feel the same way about the present Spurs team. Watching the players interact with each other, the coaches and the staff, and in their interviews with the media, the Spurs continue to be "good people". The way the guys on the bench support the players in the game is truly wonderful. I remain thankful to root for a team with good people.

Things changed a lot for the Spurs between 2014 and 2019. In my short Thanksgiving post on November 28, 2019, I tried to find some things to be thankful for in a season that ultimately ended with the COVID lockdown, the Spurs at 33-39 and missing out on the Bubble Playoffs:

"At this point in the season, it may be difficult for Spurs fans to find something to be thankful for. But remember we still have: (1) the memory of 22 straight years of winning; (2) likable and exciting players during those 22 years, including the Big Three (3), who won more regular season games and more playoff games than any threesome ever; (4) the best NBA coach ever; (5) an excellent front office, and (6) being part of a passionate and knowledgeable fan base. And, of course, the Coyote. So Happy Thanksgiving, Spurs fans."

Four years later, a portion of my 2023 Thanksgiving post remains as true now as then:

"The ex-Spurs. I am very thankful that the Spurs of our (relative) youth are still around and invested in the team. It is always great to see David Robinson in the stands at a Spurs home game, except for the fan who has to sit directly behind his 7'1" self. The Big Three also seem to be around a lot. Manu Ginobili in particular, I am confident is haunting Spurs' practices teaching the youngsters how to throw long one-handed bounce passes through traffic."

I remain thankful every time they show Manu, the Admiral or the Great Duncan it attedance for games in 2026. I am sure they enjoy seeing this team carry on the Spurs tradition.

Part of that tradition is drafting well, when the Spurs have the fop pick in the draft (the Admiral and TD), late first round picks (Tony Parker), and second round picks (Manu!). But no team can match the three consecutive drafts that produced Victor Wembanyama, Stephon Castle and Dylan Harper (plus Carter Bryant as a bonus). We really need a name for those three drafts – "Big Three" is already copyrighted. Those young players were key to last season's Thanksgiving column, written in late November 2025. That was early in the season, and thus well before anyone even suspected that the young Spurs would win the Western Conference and make the NBA for the first time in over a decade. I wrote:

"The main thing Spurs fans are thankful for: we finally have a good team again."

By contrast, this season's buzz around the league, and the team, is completely different. Even without Fox for the start of the season, and Victor's recent absence, the team has remained competitive. I am very thankful to once again turn on the game and expect a Spurs win – a feeling we haven't had in years. I am also thankful to hear the pre-game commentators on the various networks describe the team in glowing terms, also something we have not been hearing for a while."

Now we are in November 2026, with Thanksgiving upon us. Time to mention some things I am thankful for, and for this year, a few things which I am not thankful for.

As mentioned above, we can push repeat on some things to be thankful for: The Spurs are good again, remain good people while winning games, and are blessed to have the ex-Spurs out supporting the team. I will add something that his year's team has that the Big Three 'teams did not — youth! When the 2014 team won it all, it came with a sense that the Big Three era was nearing its end. Even with the disappointment of the 2026 Finals, we can be thankful to have the hope that the Spurs' latest appearance in the Finals might just be the first of many.

I am also thankful that Selena Gomez is a Spurs fan! Who knew?

On the flip side, I am NOT thankful for seeing this car on my way home from the office:

I am also less than thankful about seeing this guy when I visited our local Farmers' Market:

But back to good things. I have said many times that the key thing to playing defense is caring — a team with players that really care about stopping the other team from scoring will do all the right things to make that happen. This Spurs team clearly cares about defense, and this former coach is thankful to see how hard the team defends.

I am especially thankful to being able to look at the NBA standings every morning and see San Antonio on the top line. We will all be especially thankful if the Spurs stay on that top line through the regular season and playoffs. If they do, I might just make a big Thanksgiving dinner in late June 2027 and invite all my friends. Even those who are Knick fans.

#2745. K-beauty stocks rally as Korean brands top Amazon bestseller lists globally ahead of Black Friday

South Korean beauty stocks jumped Thursday as the country's skincare brands dominated Amazon's bestseller rankings ahead of Black Friday.

Shares of K-beauty giant APR climbed 3.77%, while cosmetic contracts manufacturers Kolmar Korea and Cosmax gained 5.46% and 2.91%, respectively.

APR shares, in particular, have rallied sharply this week, up 28%, defying a broader market sell-off in Seoul markets, with the benchmark Kospi index down 2.65% during the same period.

A cosmetic sector index tracked by Meritz Securities had gained 12% this week until Wednesday, according to a Thursday note from the brokerage.

The rally reflects renewed optimism over earnings growth following strong performances by K-beauty brands across major e-commerce platforms in the U.S. and Europe with Black Friday sales already underway, as well as increasingly attractive valuations following months of share price declines, according to Meritz.

K-beauty products topped Amazon's skincare bestseller rankings across six major markets — the U.S., the U.K., Germany, France, Spain and Italy — as of Nov. 25, according to data compiled by Meritz Securities. Other Korean beauty brands, including Biodance, Illiyoon and Anua, also ranked among the top 10 in the U.S., the data showed.

As of Thursday morning, Korean beauty products occupied several of the top spots in Amazon's U.S. beauty and personal care category, the e-commerce website showed.

Meritz Securities expects APR shares to maintain their positive momentum through the year-end, with a more substantial rebound in early 2027.

The brokerage named APR and Cosmax as its top picks in the cosmetics sector. Korean beauty brands have established an "overwhelming presence" in the bestseller rankings of major e-commerce platforms during Black Friday sales, prompting the market to reassess the growth's potential, the brokerage said.

#2743. Robots could be doing household chores in just 1-2 years, Chinese EV firm says

BEIJING — The household robot could soon become a reality, Brian Gu, co-president of Xpeng, a Chinese electric car company which has entered the humanoids space, told CNBC.

"Maybe after successful deployment into commercial settings, maybe another one to two years, we can start thinking about deploying into home settings," Gu said. "Technology is moving at a much faster clip."

Xpeng revealed its latest general-purpose humanoid robot Iron on Wednesday. The company plans to start training the robot as a store assistant in its retail car locations in the first quarter next year, after starting mass production in January. Sales are set to begin in the second quarter, with deliveries expected later in the year.

Founded in 2014 as an electric car company, Xpeng beat Tesla to rolling out driver-assist in mainland China. The Chinese automaker has launched its own artificial intelligence chips. It's also expanded into personal flying vehicles and robotics. The robotics unit this summer raised $900 million at a $6.3 billion valuation in its first funding round, led by IDG Capital.

Investors, however, appear to be a tad skeptical about the developments at Xpeng, with the downturn in the Chinese auto market also weighing on the company. Xpeng's shares, listed in both New York and Hong Kong, have dropped more than 55% this year amid broad sector declines.

China's humanoid industry has faced more regulatory scrutiny on financing in recent months after Unitree's high-profile IPO and stock slump, as well as a rush of capital into the sector. That's all raised questions about commercialization and the efficacy of the core tech.

Gu said Xpeng's robotics stands out for its rare combination of the hardware, software and real-life use cases. He's expecting to add more investors after Iron's planned commercial launch next year.

"You can always make the head talk, you can always make the leg run, you can always make the hands to do certain tasks," Gu said. "But doing a task while you're walking and answering questions at the same time, that's the most challenging part. And then doing everything well, not falling down, not getting distracted. The model has to work in sync."

Most humanoid robot startups, especially in China, focus on a portion of the tech, such as hands or the artificial intelligence-powered brain. Some in the industry also see themselves as original equipment manufacturers, with another company tying the parts together for the finished robot.

Xpeng's co-founder and CEO He Xiaopeng, a software engineer, always thought that software could solve "everything,'" Gu said. "And after he got into the EV business he realized how hard it is to make hardware ... really do the things that you want to do safely and consistently."

On Wednesday, Xpeng announced its "Turing Physical AI Architecture" for using AI and real-time decision-making tech to control cars and humanoids.

Gu told CNBC that Xpeng's robotics development only needs the driver-assist chips, which the company already has, indicating limited impact from U.S. restrictions on advanced Nvidia chip sales to China.

"Right now we don't see any change to our supply chain, and also ability to have these chips."

In 2023, Xpeng's then-head of autonomous driving, Xinzhou Wu, left to lead Nvidia's automotive chip business.

As Xpeng ramps up its humanoid robotics business, Gu said the company's existing work in autonomous driving technology translates easily to a new product line — with far higher profit margins. "It's much easier to be profitable for the robotics business than the autos business."

Robotics will likely become a meaningful part of Xpeng's business in two to three years, Gu said, with profitability coming as soon as next year. That value will "dramatically" increase with robots entering households, he said.

But Gu noted that compared to commercial environments, homes require robots to act safely around elderly people, children and pets, in addition to reacting to emergency situations. That increases requirements for battery power and performance consistency, he said.

#2742. Judge blocks FCC early-renewal process for Disney-owned ABC stations, for now

A judge has temporarily blocked the Federal Communications Commission from continuing its early renewal of a set of broadcast station licenses for Disney-owned ABC.

Judge Loren L. AliKhan of U.S. District Court in Washington granted Disney the preliminary injunction it sought and denied the FCC's attempt to dismiss the suit.

"This is not a close case," wrote the federal judge. "Given the extreme, indeed existential, nature of the possible penalty Plaintiffs face—loss of their broadcast licenses—the court concludes that early license renewal proceedings create a risk sufficient to chill a broadcaster of ordinary firmness."

The broadcast network had filed the lawsuit and followed the FCC beginning its early review of the ABC licenses. The government agency cited concerns around ABC's diversity, equity and inclusion efforts as the reason behind the start of the early renewal — years ahead of their scheduled expiration.

"Today's decision is an important victory for the First Amendment," said an ABC spokesperson.

An FCC spokesperson said in a statement that the part of the decision granting ABC's motion "is wrong on the facts and wrong on the law," the Associated Press reported.

In her ruling, AliKhan said that early license renewal proceedings came over a year into the FCC's enforcement push against ABC, starting with an investigation into Disney's employment practices in March 2025 and leading to the renewal proceedings in April this year.

ABC on Tuesday had said that it continued to "face irreparable harm" from the administration, which "has been acting" through the FCC, which is run by President Donald Trump appointee Brendan Carr.

AliKhan, a Joe Biden appointee, noted in her ruling that ABC had to change how it handled guest appearances for top shows "The View" and "Jimmy Kimmel Live!" with no candidate for public office appearing on the former and a senatorial candidate's appearance on the latter uploaded to YouTube instead of being broadcast.

"The prospect of license non-renewal has also factored into plaintiffs' calculus of when and how they cover President Trump and political topics," said the judge.

ABC had alleged that Trump and Carr have been making comments "intended to chill their protected speech," and that such comments have escalated since the 2024 presidential election.

The White House and the FCC didn't immediately respond to requests for comment from CNBC.

— CNBC's Dan Mangan contributed to the story
